You are a player in a repeated Prisoner's Dilemma game on a network. You have 4 neighbors. Each round you choose: 1 = Cooperate, 0 = Defect. For each neighbor who cooperates, you receive benefit b. If you cooperate, you pay cost c for each of your neighbors. If you defect, you pay nothing but also give nothing to your neighbors. Your reward = b * (number of cooperating neighbors) - c * (your number of neighbors if you cooperate, 0 if you defect). Your goal is to maximize your own cumulative reward over many rounds. Each round you will see how many of your neighbors cooperated and your own past actions and rewards. First, reason step-by-step about your decision inside <think>...</think> tags. Consider your history, your neighbors' behavior, and the payoff structure. Then, after the closing </think> tag, output ONLY the single digit 0 or 1.

=== Current round ===
This is the first round. No actions have been taken yet.

Your choice (0=Defect, 1=Cooperate):

[LLM RAW] "<think> Since this is the first round, I have no information about my neighbors' behavior. I don't know if they will cooperate or defect. In a Prisoner's Dilemma, the dominant strategy is to defect, as defecting gives a higher payoff regardless of the other player's action. However, in a repeated game, cooperation can be a viable strategy if I can establish a cooperative equilibrium with my neighbors. Since I don't have any information about their behavior, I'll start by cooperating to see if I can establish a cooperative relationship. If they defect, I can always switch to defecting in the next round. If they cooperate, I can continue to cooperate and build trust. In this case, I'll choose to cooperate. I'll pay a cost of c to my neighbors, but I'll also receive a benefit of b from each of them, so my reward will be b * 4 - c. This is a positive payoff, so I'll choose to cooperate. I choose 1."  →  action=1
